{{Elections in North Carolina sidebar}}
The '''2024 North Carolina House of Representatives election''' was held on November 5, 2024, to elect all 120 members to [[North Carolina]]'s [[North Carolina House of Representatives|House of Representatives]]. The election coincided with [[2024 North Carolina elections|elections]] for other offices, including for the [[2024 United States presidential election in North Carolina|U.S. President]], [[2024 United States House of Representatives elections in North Carolina|U.S. House of Representatives]], [[2024 North Carolina Council of State elections|Council of State]], and [[2024 North Carolina Senate election|State Senate]].<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|url=https://ballotpedia.org/North_Carolina_House_of_Representatives_elections,_2024|title=North Carolina House of Representatives elections, 2024|publisher=Ballotpedia|accessdate=October 25, 2023}}</ref> The [[Partisan primary|primary election]] was held on March 5, 2024. Ahead of the election, districts were redrawn and passed the General Assembly in House Bill 898.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|web|url=https://webservices.ncleg.gov/ViewBillDocument/2023/7590/0/Edition%203%20-%2019%20x%2036%20Map|title=H898, Edition 3|publisher=North Carolina General Assembly|date=October 25, 2023}}</ref> Democrats gained one seat, breaking the Republicans' [[supermajority]].<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|title=Democrats Break the GOP Supermajority in the Legislature–But Barely |url=https://www.theassemblync.com/politics/democrats-republicans-nc-assembly-supermajority/ |access-date=April 29, 2025 |publisher=The Assemby NC |date=November 6, 2024}}</ref>

==Partisan background==
In the 2020 Presidential Election, Trump won in 70 North Carolina House districts, while Biden won 50. As the 2024 election approached, Democrats held three districts where Trump won: District 35 in suburban Raleigh, District 73 around Concord, and District 115 in rural Buncombe County. Republicans, on the other hand, controlled five districts where Biden led: District 5 in Eastern NC, District 24 in Wilson County, District 25 in Nash County, District 32 in Granville and Vance Counties, and District 98 in northern Mecklenburg County. 
[[File:2020 North Carolina Presidential Election by State House District.svg|350px|thumb|center|{{col-begin}} {{col-2}} '''Biden''' {{legend|#B9D7FF|40–50%}} {{legend|#86B6F2|50–60%}} {{legend|#4389E3|60–70%}} {{legend|#1666CB|70–80%}} {{legend|#0645B4|80–90%}}  {{col-2}} '''Trump'''  {{legend|#E27F90|50–60%}} {{legend|#CC2F4A|60–70%}} {{legend|#D40000|70–80%}} {{col-end}}]]

*[[North Carolina's 42nd House district|District 42]]: [[Marvin W. The new 1st district includes all of [[Chowan County, North Carolina|Chowan]], [[Currituck County, North Carolina|Currituck]], [[Perquimans County, North Carolina|Perquimans]], [[Tyrrell County, North Carolina|Tyrrell]], and [[Washington County, North Carolina|Washington]] counties, as well as portions of northwestern [[Dare County, North Carolina|Dare County]]. It includes the home of incumbent Republican [[Ed Goodwin]], who has represented the 1st district since 2019.

The new 32nd district includes all of [[Granville County, North Carolina|Granville County]], as well as portions of northern [[Vance County, North Carolina|Vance County]]. It includes the home of incumbent Republican [[Frank Sossamon]], who has represented the 32nd district since 2023.

====District 33====
{{Infobox election

The new 33rd district includes portions of eastern [[Wake County, North Carolina|Wake County]]. It includes the home of incumbent Democrat [[Rosa Gill]], who has represented the 33rd district since 2009. On December 4, 2023, Gill announced her intent to retire after eight terms.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|last=Campbell |first=Colin |date=December 4, 2023 |title=Two veteran NC lawmakers plan to retire |url=https://www.wunc.org/politics/2023-12-04/two-veteran-nc-lawmakers-plan-to-retire |access-date=January 22, 2024 |work=[[WUNC (FM)|WUNC]]}}</ref> Fellow Democrat and Wake County Board of Education member [[Monika Johnson-Hostler]] won the open seat.

The new 42nd district includes portions of northwestern [[Cumberland County, North Carolina|Cumberland County]]. It includes the home of incumbent Democrat [[Marvin W. Lucas|Marvin Lucas]], who has represented the 42nd district since 2001. On December 6, 2023, Lucas announced his intent to retire after twelve terms.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|last=Bajpai |first=Avi |date=December 6, 2023 |title='The dean' of NC House Democrats won't run again. Here's who he wants to replace him. |url=https://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/article282716663.html |access-date=January 22, 2024 |work=[[The News & Observer]]}}</ref>

The new 52nd district includes all of [[Richmond County, North Carolina|Richmond County]] and portions of southern [[Moore County, North Carolina|Moore County]]. It includes the home of incumbent Republican [[Ben Moss]], who has represented the 52nd district and its predecessors since 2021. Initially, on December 20, 2022, Moss announced his intent to retire after two terms and announced a run for [[2024 North Carolina Council of State elections#Commissioner_of_Labor|Labor Commissioner]],<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|date=December 20, 2022 |title=Moss announces plan to run for Labor Commissioner |url=https://www.carolinajournal.com/moss-announces-plan-to-run-for-labor-commissioner/ |access-date=January 22, 2024 |work=[[Carolina Journal]]}}</ref> but on July 19, 2023, he withdrew from that race and announced he would run for a third term.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|date=July 19, 2023 |title=North Carolina state Rep. Ben Moss won't run for labor commissioner in 2024 |url=https://apnews.com/article/north-carolina-labor-commissioner-race-ben-moss-610e6e828f76c0924883aeefcb21ffd1 |access-date=January 22, 2024 |work=[[Associated Press]]}}</ref>

}}

On October 31, 2023, Faircloth announced his intent to retire after seven terms.<ref>{{#invoke:cite|news|last=Johnson |first=Paul B. |date=December 8, 2023 |title=Faircloth won't seek reelection |url=https://www.hpenews.com/news/faircloth-won-t-seek-reelection/article_cc9e3def-8b86-5723-9d4c-3d99e7e92045.html |access-date=January 22, 2024 |work=[[High Point Enterprise]]}}</ref> Faircloth resigned his seat on September 6, 2024 and Republican nominee and former Representative [[John Blust]] was appointed to the seat on September 17, 2024.

The new 120th district includes all of [[Cherokee County, North Carolina|Cherokee]], [[Clay County, North Carolina|Clay]], [[Graham County, North Carolina|Graham]], and [[Macon County, North Carolina|Macon]] counties. It includes the home of incumbent Republican [[Karl Gillespie]], who has represented the 120th district since 2021.
